# CORE-CAP-SPECIALTY — consolidated source profiles

Extends existing canonical owner and all prior addenda. Every clause below applies only to its listed source profiles; it is not a universal clinical rule. No new service, clinical approval or implementation readiness.

## condition-history

The existing specialty owner holds longitudinal domain projections; authoritative diagnoses, allergies, tests and treatments stay with shared owners. Source-scoped disease and multisystem requirements are preserved as profiles rather than new services.

Acceptance additions: Preserve suspected versus confirmed states, chronology, absence versus unknown, source authority and condition-specific scales. Combining a timeline must not silently create clinical facts.

## specialty-hubs

Existing specialty orchestration profiles compose shared capabilities. A hub does not absorb AI, CDS, Twin, research, identity or interoperability ownership. Each source domain retains its own scope and source-specific requirements.

Acceptance additions: Preserve domain/life-stage context, escalation, transitions and specialty workflows. Source MVP/foundation claims do not expand the approved minimal vertical slice or establish implemented readiness.

## specialty-cores

Specialty core engines are domain orchestration/profile requirements under the existing specialty owner, not independent duplicate services. Preserve disease, lifespan, clinical data and interdisciplinary context; authoritative records and clinical actions stay with the shared capabilities they reference.

Acceptance additions: Preserve source-specific privacy and guardian controls, sensitive segmentation, transition care and clinical escalation. MVP HIGH and foundation wording do not prove readiness or expand the initial slice. Require exact source-to-authority contracts before runtime integration.
